L37: ## 1 Executive Summary
L38: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L39: Proposed Trade Name:	Grafapex
Established Name:	Treosulfan
Also Known As:	Treosulphan, dihydroxybusulfan, CAS # 299-75-2, Ovastat®
Chemical Name:	1,2,3,4-butanetcol, 1,4-dimethanesulfonate; threitol 1,4-dimethanesulfonate; threitol 1,4-bismethanesulfonate; L-threitol 1,4-bis(methanesulfonate); (2S,3S)-2,3-dihydroxybutane-1,4-diyl dimethanesulfaonte; (2S,3S)-(-)-1,4,-Di(mesyloxy)-2,3-butanediol
Molecular Formula:	C_6H_{14}O_8S_2	Chemical Structure:	
Molecular Weight:	278.3 g/mol
Dosage Forms:	Injection, lyophilized (1 gm, 5 gm)
Therapeutic Class:	Antineoplastic
Chemical Class:	Small molecule
Pharmacologic Class:	Alkylating agent
Mechanism of Action:	Treosulfan is an alkylating agent. DNA alkylation is thought to be responsible for the cytotoxic activities of treosulfan. Treosulfan depleted hematopoietic stem cells and showed immunosuppressive and antitumor activity in mouse models of leukemia.
L40: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L41: Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness (SEE) was established with one adequate and well-controlled clinical investigation and confirmatory evidence.
L42: MC-FludT.14/L Trial II (NCT00822393) was a multicenter, randomized, active-controlled trial comparing treosulfan to busulfan in combination with fludarabine as a preparative regimen for allogeneic HSCT. Eligible patients included adults 18 to 70 years old with AML in complete remission or MDS, Karnofsky performance status &gt; 60%, and hematopoietic cell transplantation comorbidity index [HCT CI] score &gt; 2 for patients &lt; 50 years old. The patients were randomized to receive treosulfan 10 g/m2 daily on days -4, -3 and -2 or to busulfan 0.8 mg/kg every 6 hours on days -4 and -3 in combination with fludarabine 30 mg/m2 daily on days -6, -5, -4, -3 and -2, and hematopoietic stem cell transplantation on day 0. Antithymocyte globulin was administered as well to patients with unrelated donors.
L43: The study was designed as a noninferiority trial with a primary endpoint of event-free survival
L44: (EFS) with 2 years of follow-up after transplantation. Events were defined as disease relapse, graft failure, or death. The noninferiority margin on the hazard ratio scale was 1.3. If noninferiority was demonstrated, then the primary endpoint would be tested for superiority. Overall survival (OS) was a secondary endpoint, but there was no alpha allocated to testing OS.
L45: The trial was stopped at the second interim analysis when EFS noninferiority was achieved. Since the trial continued enrolling until the results of the interim analysis were known, an additional "last analysis" was conducted using all enrolled patients (N = 570). Based on the last analysis, the Applicant concluded that the results supported noninferiority for EFS, and in sequential testing, superiority of treosulfan for EFS (based on 224 events). Superiority of treosulfan for EFS was not demonstrated at the second interim analysis, though the boundary for superiority based on the updated number of EFS events was crossed at the last analysis. However, FDA did not consider the trial to have demonstrated superiority of treosulfan for EFS at the last analysis, as this analysis was not pre-planned and had been requested by FDA.
L46: During review of the original NDA submission, a Complete Response was issued on 30 July 2021 as the review team was unable to confirm the results of the primary endpoint analysis since the raw data needed to verify the components of the EFS endpoint were incomplete. In the subsequent resubmissions (21 April 2022, 30 April 2024), the Applicant submitted all available CBC data and some bone marrow results. In the current resubmission, the Applicant submitted the results of all bone marrow results including original reports.
L47: For FDA's analysis of efficacy, the analysis set consisted of all randomized patients. This included 570 patients with a median age of 70 years (range 31, 70); 61% were male. Race and ethnicity data were not collected. Sixty-four percent of patients had AML. Ninety-seven percent of patients received peripheral blood stem cells.
L48: Study MC FludT.14/L Trial II was considered a positive study based on the primary endpoint of EFS (non-inferiority p-value = 0.0000164 as compared to an efficacy boundary of 0.000149). Based on FDA-adjudication of EFS events, the hazard ratio for EFS was 0.65 (95% CI: 0.50, 0.85) in the ITT population. However, the definition of EFS used by the Applicant reflected a composite of efficacy (relapse and death) and safety (graft failure) measures. In the accepted definition of EFS for AML, graft failure is not considered an efficacy event. For patients with AML in CR, the applicable endpoint would be relapse-free survival (RFS). For patients with MDS not in CR, achievement of remission might reflect the effectiveness of treatment, but the protocol definition of EFS does not take into account failure to achieve remission and the data collection was not sufficient to reliably adjudicate achievement of CR in the MDS population. Therefore, the clinical meaningfulness of the EFS endpoint as defined in the protocol is unclear for both the AML and MDS populations. Additional analyses of EFS and related endpoints (including RFS) were conducted to explore the robustness of the result to definition and population. The results of these additional analyses consistently favored the treosulfan arm.
L49: (b) (4)
L50: While Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II was not designed to test OS, this endpoint is the gold standard for establishing efficacy in oncology. Thus, FDA relied on OS, defined as the time from randomization until death from any cause, to establish the efficacy of treosulfan. The HR for OS (Cox proportional hazards model, stratified by donor type and risk group) compared to busulfan was 0.67 (95% CI: 0.51, 0.90) in the ITT population, 0.73 (95% CI: 0.51, 1.06) in patients with AML, and 0.64 (95% CI: 0.40, 1.02) in patients with MDS. Despite the limitations of the study design and available data, the results appear consistent with superiority overall. However, since there was no prespecified alpha-spending plan for OS, no p-values will be reported.
L51: Supportive evidence for the effectiveness of treosulfan as a component of a preparative regimen was based on the clinical evidence from the dose-finding study MC-FludT.6/L which accrued a total of 55 patients, including 19 with AML and 6 with MDS. Study MC-FludT.6/L achieved its primary objective to evaluate the feasibility and tolerability of different dose levels of treosulfan prior to allogeneic HSCT. In addition, the 1-year OS outcomes for patients with hematologic malignancies who received the treosulfan-based preparative regimen at 10 g/m2/dose, 12 g/m2/dose, and 14 g/m2/dose were 70%, 78%, and 53%, respectively. Although FDA does not usually use landmark OS in a single-arm trial to support efficacy given the potential for bias, the findings in MC-FludT.6/L were reproducible in two dose cohorts (10 g/m2 and 12 g/m2), the results in the 10 g/m2 cohort were consistent with that of the treosulfan arm of MC-FludT.14/L Trial II (1-year OS: 79%), and the results in the 14 g/m2 cohort were also consistent with the results in the treosulfan arm of MC-FludT.14/L Trial I (1-year OS: 67%). The consistency of these findings reduces concerns for bias and supports the conclusion that the 10 g/m2/dose treosulfan is effective as part of a preparative regimen.
L52: On the basis of biology of AML and MDS and the mechanism of action of treosulfan, the efficacy of treosulfan 10 g/m2/dose can be extrapolated from the studies in adults to pediatric patients.
L53: the recommended dose for pediatric patients with AML or MDS is 10 g/m2/dose of treosulfan based on a full extrapolation of efficacy from MC-FludT.14/L Trial II and PopPK approach.
L54: In summary, MC-FludT.14/L Trial II was a positive study based on its primary endpoint, and the overall results demonstrate the efficacy of 10 g/m2/dose treosulfan as a component of conditioning regimen prior to allogeneic HSCT. Treosulfan-based preparative regimens prior to allogeneic HSCT in patients with AML or MDS result in a survival advantage (HR 0.67 [95% CI: 0.51, 0.90]) compared to busulfan-based preparative regimens. The recommended indication for treosulfan (10 g/m2/dose) is: "for use in combination with fludarabine as a preparative regimen for allogeneic HSCT in adult and pediatric patients 1 year of age and older with AML (1.1) ... [or]... MDS (1.2)."
L55: ## 1.3. Benefit-Risk Assessment
L56: ## Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment
L57: AML and MDS have high rates of morbidity and mortality, and allogeneic hematopoietic stem cell transplantation (HSCT) is required for curative therapy in patients with high-risk disease. Currently, there are no drugs approved for use as a preparative regimen prior to allogeneic HSCT for AML or MDS, and regimens are not standardized. There is a need for additional conditioning regimen options for patients with AML and MDS proceeding to allogeneic HSCT.
L58: MC-FludT.14/L Trial II (NCT00822393) was a multicenter, randomized, active-controlled trial comparing treosulfan to busulfan in combination with fludarabine as a preparative regimen for allogeneic HSCT. Eligible patients included adults 18 to 70 years old with AML in complete remission or MDS, Karnofsky performance status &gt; 60%, and hematopoietic cell transplantation comorbidity index [HCT CI] score &gt; 2 for patients &lt; 50 years of age. Patients were randomized to receive treosulfan 10 g/m2 daily on days -4, -3 and -2 or to busulfan 0.8 mg/kg every 6 hours on days -4 and -3 in combination with fludarabine 30 mg/m2 daily on days -6, -5, -4, -3 and -2, and hematopoietic stem cell transplantation on day 0. Antithymocyte globulin was administered as well to patients with unrelated donors.
L59: The study was designed as a noninferiority trial with a primary endpoint of event-free survival (EFS) with 2 years of follow-up after transplantation. Events were defined as disease relapse, graft failure, or death. The noninferiority margin on the hazard ratio scale was 1.3. If noninferiority was demonstrated, then the primary endpoint would be tested for superiority. Overall survival (OS) was a secondary endpoint, but there was no alpha allocated to testing OS.
L60: Study MC FludT.14/L Trial II was considered a positive study based on the primary endpoint of EFS (non-inferiority p-value = 0.0000164 as compared to an efficacy boundary of 0.000149). Based on FDA-adjudication, EFS showed a hazard ratio (HR) of 0.65 (95% CI: 0.50, 0.85) in the ITT population. However, EFS as defined by the Applicant reflects a composite of efficacy (relapse and death) and safety (graft failure) measures, and the clinical meaningfulness of this definition is unclear for both the AML and MDS populations. Additional analyses of EFS and related endpoints (including RFS) were conducted to explore the robustness of the result to definition and population. The results of these additional analyses consistently favored the treosulfan arm which supported the conclusion of a positive trial, (b) (4)
L61: Although Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II was not designed to test OS, this endpoint is the gold standard for establishing efficacy in oncology. Therefore, FDA relied on OS, defined as the time from randomization until death from any cause, to establish the efficacy of treosulfan. The HR for OS (Cox proportional hazards model, stratified by donor type and risk group) compared to busulfan was 0.67 (95% CI: 0.51, 0.90) in the ITT
L62: population, 0.73 (95% CI: 0.51, 1.06) in patients with AML, and 0.64 (95% CI: 0.40, 1.02) in patients with MDS. Despite the limitations of the study design and available data, the results appear consistent with superiority overall. However, since there was no prespecified alpha-spending plan for OS, no p-values will be reported.
L63: Adverse events with treosulfan were similar to those observed with busulfan-based conditioning. Common TEAEs (\ge 20\%) included musculoskeletal pain, stomatitis, pyrexia, nausea, edema, infection, and vomiting. Of those, musculoskeletal pain (12% higher), edema (11% higher), and infection (5% higher) occurred at a rate at least 5% higher in the treosulfan arm than the busulfan arm. The most common grade 3-5 AEs (\ge 5\%) in the treosulfan arm were febrile neutropenia, infection, hypertension, stomatitis, and musculoskeletal pain. Of those, infection occurred at a rate of &gt;5\% compared to the busulfan arm (6% higher in the treosulfan arm).
L64: MC-FludT.14/L Trial I which randomized patients to treosulfan-based conditioning using 14 \text{ g}/\text{m}^2/dose vs busulfan-based conditioning was terminated early due to delayed hematopoietic recovery and increase early treatment-related mortality (10.2% vs 5.3% in the treosulfan and busulfan arms, respectively). FDA's analysis of the final data cut (1-year follow-up) showed no evidence of a treatment effect for this higher treosulfan dose compared to the busulfan-based regimen for the overall population (OS HR 1.15 [95% CI: 0.78, 1.69]) or when assessed individually for patients with AML or with MDS. The results of this study also confirmed that higher doses of treosulfan result in increased early morbidity and mortality in addition to higher transplant-related and non-relapse mortality. These findings warrant inclusion of a Warning and Precaution in the USPI since the 14 \text{ g}/\text{m}^2 dose is published in the literature and approved for the pediatric population in some countries.
L65: Based on the biology of AML and MDS and the mechanism of action of treosulfan, the efficacy of treosulfan 10 \text{ g}/\text{m}^2/dose can be extrapolated from the studies in adults to pediatric patients, and safety data for treosulfan in combination with fludarabine are available for patients ages 1 year and older. (b) (4)
L66: the recommended dose for pediatric patients with AML or MDS is 10 \text{ g}/\text{m}^2/dose of treosulfan based on a full extrapolation of efficacy from MC-FludT.14/L Trial II and Population PK approach.
L67: Given the observed OS benefit and with risk mitigation strategies in place in labeling, the clinical benefit of 10 \text{ g}/\text{m}^2/dose treosulfan in combination with fludarabine as a preparative regimen for allogeneic HSCT appears to outweigh the risks in adult and pediatric patients 1 year of age or older with AML or MDS.
L68: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation [NDA 214759]
## Grafapex (treosulfan)
L69: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	Acute myeloid leukemia (AML) and high-risk myelodysplastic syndrome (MDS) have high rates of morbidity and mortality.
Allogeneic HSCT is required for curative therapy of high-risk AML and IPSS-R intermediate, high, and very high risk MDS.
AML and MDS are serious and life-threatening diseases.
Current Treatment Options	There are no drugs approved for us as a preparative regimen prior to allogeneic HSCT for AML or MDS.
There is no consensus regarding choice of preparative regimen and there are no standardized guidelines for which patients should receive reduced-intensity or myeloablative regimens.
There is a need for additional conditioning regimen options for patients with AML and MDS proceeding to allogeneic HSCT.
Benefit	MC-FludT.14/L Trial II (NCT00822393) was a multicenter, randomized, active-controlled trial comparing treosulfan 10 g/m2/dose to busulfan in combination with fludarabine as a preparative regimen for allogeneic HSCT in patients with AML in CR or with MDS. A total of 570 patients were randomized.
The study was positive based on its primary endpoint of EFS: HR 0.65 (95% CI: 0.50, 0.85). However, the applicability of this endpoint as defined in the protocol to the AML and MDS subpopulations was unclear.
Efficacy was established based on OS: HR 0.67 (95% CI: 0.51, 0.90) in the ITT population. Results for patients with AML and MDS were consistent with those for the overall study: OS HR 0.73 (95% CI: 0.51, 1.06) in patients with AML and 0.64 (95% CI: 0.40, 1.02) in patients with MDS.
Efficacy of 10 g/m2/dose of treosulfan in pediatric patients with AML or MDS is extrapolated based on the adult study and supported by safety data in pediatric patients aged 1 year and older.
Treosulfan 10 g/m2/dose in combination with fludarabine is an effective preparative regimen prior to allogeneic HSCT for patients ≥ 1 year old with AML or MDS.
L70: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation [NDA 214759]
## Grafapex (treosulfan)
L71: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Risk and Risk Management	Fatal adverse reactions occurred in 1.5% of patients who received treosulfan.
The most common (\ge 20\%) adverse reactions were musculoskeletal pain, stomatitis, pyrexia, nausea, edema, infection, and vomiting.
Grade 3 or 4 nonhematological laboratory abnormalities included increased GGT, increased bilirubin, increased ALT, increased AST, and increased creatinine.
MC-FludT.14/L Trial I demonstrated substantial evidence of increased morbidity and mortality with 14 \text{ g}/\text{m}^2/\text{day} of treosulfan.
The safety profile of treosulfan 10 \text{ g}/\text{m}^2/\text{dose} appears similar to that of busulfan, but significant safety issues with the 14 \text{ g}/\text{m}^2/\text{dose} regimen including higher treatment-related mortality warrants inclusion of a Warning and Precaution in the USPI since this dose is published in the literature and approved for use in the pediatric population in some countries.

L85: ## 3.2. Summary of Presubmission/Submission Regulatory Activity
L86: Please refer to the FDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation of NDA 214759 (30 July 2021) for summary of presubmission/submission regulatory activity prior to July 2021.
L87: - NDA 214759 received a Complete Response Letter on 7/30/2021- FDA noted that the Applicant had not provided substantial evidence of effectiveness of treosulfan as part of a preparative regimen for allogenic hematopoietic stem cell transplant. FDA was unable to confirm the primary endpoint of EFS because all complete blood cell (CBC) and bone marrow results were not submitted to support adjudication of the endpoint. Additionally, because of the missing CBC and marrow results, FDA was not able to complete an assessment of whether the results of the study would support the indications for treatment of patients with AML and for treatment of patients with MDS. To address this issue, FDA asked the Applicant to submit the CBC and marrow results through the complete assessment period for EFS for MC-FLudT.14/L Trial II. FDA also noted that [REDACTED] (b) (4) OS cannot be used as the sole basis for approval since the statistical analysis plan for Study MC-FLudT-14/L trial II neither include Type 1 error for OS nor alpha allocation for multiple OS analyses. Since no alpha was reserved for independent OS analysis and the EFS endpoint was unable to be verified due to lack of CBC and marrow data noted above, FDA concluded that without verification of the EFS analysis, any OS analysis could only be considered exploratory. To independently address the issues with OS, FDA recommended submitting data from an adequate and well-controlled trial of treosulfan vs busulfan with a prespecified analysis of OS for each indication with control of Type 1 error.
- Type A Meeting 11/23/2021- FDA and the Applicant discussed the Applicant's plan to evaluate and present additional bone marrow and CBC results gathered through the complete assessment period for EFS via the MC-FLudT.14/L Trial II. FDA again asked for clarification for how missing assessments were handled. FDA also noted that there is no acceptable definition for EFS in MDS and that RFS more accurately reflects the AML patient's clinical circumstances. Discussion was also had on clinical studies to address clinical pharmacology issues include QTc assessments.
- Response to CR submitted 4/21/2022- Applicant submitted updated laboratory and chimerism data with sensitivity analyses aimed at addressing missing assessment data
L88: through various censoring rules for EFS in response to CR comments discussed at the Type A meeting of 11/23/21.
L89: - Acknowledgement Incomplete response letter sent 5/16/2022- Letter noted that the Applicant had not yet provided sufficient evidence of effectiveness of treosulfan as a part of a preparative regimen for allogeneic hematopoietic stem cell transplantation for adult and pediatric patients with AML or MDS. FDA asked the Applicant to update all files dependent on the newly collected laboratory data include the adtte.xpt files. However, FDA also noted continued missing data with respect to bone marrow chimerism results and assay validation.
- Second Response to CR submitted 8/10/2022- Applicant submitted updated lab files with updated dependencies as noted in the Acknowledgement Incomplete response letter sent 5/16/2022 or submitted additional files with new names to preserve the files from the initial submission. The Applicant also submitted data detailing site specific chimerism assays. Lastly, the Applicant provided an updated adtte.xpt file with the new sensitivity analyses.
- Second Acknowledgement Incomplete Response Letter sent 9/15/2022- FDA noted that detailed review of the newly submitted data continued to identify inconsistencies and a substantial extent of missing assessments. Specifically, FDA noted that blast values presented in updated lab data sets indicated that MRD assays rather than a morphological assessment of marrow blasts were erroneously being reported. Additionally, FDA identified 179 missing day +28 or Day +100 protocol required disease assessments. Therefore, FDA concluded that the Applicant had yet to provide substantial evidence of effectiveness.
- Type A meeting 12/14/2022- FDA provided additional feedback on the Applicant's proposed approach to address the deficiencies noted in FDA's Acknowledge Incomplete Response Letter issued on September 15, 2022, and all other issues noted in the CRL and in the Information Request dated August 31, 2022. FDA noted that based on the issues identified during reviews of your submissions dated April 21, and August 10, 2022, we have no confidence in the marrow data submitted in the datasets, and source documentation for marrow examinations from only 50 patients would not be sufficient to allay FDA's concerns about data integrity. Therefore, the Applicant would need to submit the source documentation for all marrow examinations that were performed through the complete assessment period for EFS for all subjects in MCFLudT.14/L Trial II.
- Third Response to CR submitted 4/30/2024- In response to the issues raised by the Agency, the Applicant performed a systematic review of all source documentation of bone marrow evaluations and provided the source documentation in original language and with English translations for all subjects from Study MC-FLudT.14/L Trial II. The results of this effort, along with specific responses from the Applicant to each of the issues raised in the Acknowledgment Incomplete Response Letter from September 15, 2022, were provided in the current submission.

L118: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L119: Excerpted from NDA 214759 Clinical Pharmacology Re-submission Memo (31 October 2024):
L120: " The Office of Clinical Pharmacology did not recommend approval then because of issues regarding the veracity of the primary study endpoint. A summary of key review issues and recommendations from the original clinical pharmacology review of NDA 214759 is provided below:
L121: REVIEW ISSUE	RECOMMENDATIONS / COMMENTS
General dosing instructions	The recommended dosage regimen of treosulfan is 10 g/m2/day for 3 consecutive days (Days -4, -3, and -2) in combination with fludarabine 30 mg/m2/day for 5 consecutive days (Days -6 through -2) prior to allogeneic hematopoietic stem cell transplantation (Day 0).
Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	Pediatric Patients \ge1 year of age: The recommended dosage regimen of treosulfan is the same for all patients \ge1 year of age. Efficacy in pediatric patients \ge1 year of age was extrapolated from adults and dose selection was supported by PK and safety data from Studies MC-FludT.16/NM and MC-FludT.17/M. Treosulfan exposure after administration of 10 g/m2 in pediatric patients with BSA <0.7 m2 and with BSA 0.7 to <1.1 m2 are 11% and 5% higher, respectively, compared to adults. Exposure-response analyses for safety did not indicate that an 11% increase in exposures would result in significant increase in the probability of common toxicities.
Renal Impairment: No dosage modification is recommended for patients with mild renal impairment. An additional study will be necessary to identify a safe dose of treosulfan for patients with moderate hepatic impairment. A study in patients with severe renal impairment is not feasible and will not be required.
Hepatic Impairment: No dosage modification is recommended for patients with mild hepatic impairment. Studies in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment are not needed, as these patients are typically excluded from transplantation.
L122: "The only new data submitted in this re-submission were in vitro metabolism study results which determined that treosulfan is metabolized by CYP2D6 and its monoepoxide intermediate is metabolized by CYP2C8. The labeling has been updated to reflect this information. In vitro metabolism studies for other metabolites are currently ongoing.
L123: This NDA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective."
L124: Excerpted from Addendum to Clinical Pharmacology Re-submission Memo (27 November 2024):
L125: "The previous review indicated the need for the following PMR: Conduct an in vitro study to evaluate treosulfan and epoxide metabolites as substrates of major cytochrome P450 enzymes consistent with FDA guidance.
L126: The in vitro assessment of treosulfan metabolism showed involvement of CYP2D6 (t_{1/2} with rCYP2D6 = 108 \text{ min}) and was noted in the previous review. Based on an updated communication with the Applicant ..., we have determined that the above PMR is no longer needed."
L127: "Based on a review of the submitted reports of the in vitro studies, the monoepoxide ... and diepoxide metabolites ... are not meaningfully metabolized by CYP enzymes. Of note, the t_{1/2} values for positive control compounds for the respective rCYPs were very short. No labeling update is needed based on these results."

L152: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical and Evaluation
L153: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L154: ## 8.1.1. Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II
L155: ## Trial Design
L156: Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II was a phase III open-label, randomized clinical trial to compare treosulfan-based conditioning therapy with busulfan-based reduced-intensity conditioning (RIC) prior to allogeneic haematopoietic stem cell transplantation in patients with AML or MDS considered ineligible for myeloablative conditioning regimens. The first patient was enrolled on 13-Jun-2014 and the last patient completed 25-Jan-2018.
L157: Per statistical analysis plan, "MC-FludT.14/L was designed as a multicentre, multinational, randomised, active-controlled, group-sequential phase III trial to show non-inferiority of Treosulfan-based conditioning therapy compared to Busulfan-based reduced-intensity conditioning (RIC) prior to allogeneic haematopoietic stem cell transplantation in patients with Acute Myeloid Leukaemia (AML) or Myelodysplastic syndromes (MDS) considered ineligible to standard conditioning regimens."
L158: The study was designed as a non-inferiority trial with a primary endpoint of event-free survival (EFS) within 2 years. The study population consisted of adult patients with AML or MDS who were not eligible for myeloablative conditioning regimens. Patients were randomized to receive either reduced-intensity conditioning with busulfan and fludarabine, or the treosulfan and fludarabine.
L159: ## Treatment Plan Overview
L160: Patients were randomized to receive either RIC with the control (reference) arm of busulfan + fludarabine, or the test arm of treosulfan + fludarabine (Table 2).
L161: **Table 2: Treatment Overview of MC-FludT.14/L Trial II**
L162: Treatment	Day -6	-5	-4	-3	-2	-1	0	+1	+3	+6
Treosulfan (IV, 10 g/m2 within 120 minutes) – test arm	X	X	X	
Busulfan (IV, 0.8 mg/kg within 120 minutes) - control	X	X	
Fludarabine (IV, 30 mg/m2 within 30 minutes)	X	X	X	X	X	
ATG-S-Fresenius / Grafalon (IV, 10 mg/kg in MUD) – Germany, Hungary, Italy, Poland	X	X	
ATG-Thymoglobulin (IV, 2.5 mg/kg in MUD) – France	X	X	
Allogeneic stem cell transplantation	X	
Cyclosporine-A (IV, PO, dose level adjusted)	X	X	X...	X...	X...
Methotrexate (IV, mg/m2/day)	15	10	10
Source Data: Clinical Protocol, adapted from Appendix 16-16.1.1
L163: All patients received fludarabine (30 mg/m2/d) on days -6 to -2. Patients on the control arm received busulfan (0.8 mg/kg) on days -4 and -3. Patients receiving busulfan received anti-seizure prophylaxis. Patients on the treatment arm received treosulfan (10 mg/m2/d) on days -4 to -2. Additionally, patients who received a MUD received ATG. The type of ATG and dosing varied by country.
L164: GVHD prophylaxis was identical in both arms. All patients received level-adapted cyclosporine from Day -1 through Day +100 and methotrexate on days +1, +3, and +6, with folic acid rescue.
L165: Please refer to the FDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation of NDA 214759 (30 July 2021) for further details regarding the study design.
L166: ## Study Endpoints
L167: Please refer to the FDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation of NDA 214759 (30 July 2021) for the full list of endpoints and results. This review focuses on the assessment of the event-free survival (primary endpoint) and the overall survival.
L168: ## Selected Protocol-Specified Endpoints
L169: ## **Primary Endpoint: Event-Free Survival (within 24 months post-transplantation)**
L170: Per the statistical analysis plan, the primary efficacy endpoint was “event-free survival (EFS) within 2 years after transplantation measured from time of end of HSCT (= day 0) to time of event. The associated time span is defined as the interval from day 0 to relapse, graft failure or death (whatever occurs first). The date of the event is either the documented date of relapse/progression or the date of death or the date of primary (date of examination of neutrophilic granulocytes) or secondary graft failure (date of examination of neutrophilic granulocytes).”
L171: ## **Statistical Reviewer's Comment**
L172: *The following key issues were identified with event-free survival, as specified in the statistical analysis plan:*
L173: - **Day Zero: Transplantation (HSCT).** The period of regulatory interest starts from randomization.
- **Timeframe:** Within 24 months. The period of regulatory interest is not restricted to 24 months.
- **Events:** Sufficient data to confirm the outcomes were missing in the initial submissions. FDA adjudicated the events with the additional submitted data. See the Additional Endpoints per FDA Assessment section below for further details.
- **Variables used in the Cox-proportional hazards model:** risk group, donor type, center. Including center is not appropriate (see the Statistical Issues section).
L174: 5. **Interpretation:** Interpretation of the treatment effect based on event-free survival is challenging. The Applicant utilized a definition for EFS in this trial that was not standard and had not been discussed with FDA prior to the study. For AML studies, FDA has accepted an EFS endpoint that represent the time from randomization until the time of an event. Events are considered induction treatment failure, relapse for those who have induction treatment success, or death. The eligibility criteria for this study required patients with AML to be in remission prior to transplant. Therefore, for the AML patients a Relapse-Free Survival (RFS) endpoint would have more accurately reflected the events within this clinical context. This analysis was requested as discussed below. Additional issues with the Applicant's AML EFS event definition were the inclusion of primary or secondary graft failure as events and the inclusion of cytogenetic and molecular relapse events without evidence of morphological relapse.
L175: EFS is not an established clinical benefit endpoint for new drugs for MDS and has not been used for regulatory purposes in MDS. As with the AML patients, the Applicant's MDS EFS event definition included the use of primary or secondary graft failure as events, which is a nonstandard EFS event. Moreover, the MDS EFS definition did not include treatment failure as an event even though most MDS patients were not in remission (and were not required to be) at that time of transplant. Additionally, patients were not formally assessed for response after transplant with post-transplant assessments focusing on engraftment instead. Therefore, the interpretation of relapse events for the MDS patient was unclear since the patients were not formally assessed for remission.
L176: Issues 1-4 were addressed by FDA by defining a different endpoint: event-free survival (from randomization). See the Additional Endpoints per FDA Assessment section below for further details.
L177: ## **Overall Survival (within 24 months post-transplantation)**
L178: Per statistical analysis plan, overall survival was defined as "the time length between day 0 and the date of death due to any cause within the 24-month study period (i.e. excluding post-surveillance evaluation)."
L179: **Day Zero:** Transplantation (HSCT)
L180: **Timeframe:** within 24 months
L181: ## **Statistical Reviewer's Comment**
L182: The following key issues were identified with the overall survival, as specified in the statistical analysis plan:
L183: - **Day Zero:** Transplantation (HSCT). The period of regulatory interest starts from randomization.
L184: 2. **Timeframe:** Within 24 months. The period of regulatory interest is not restricted to 24 months.
L185: These issues were addressed by FDA by defining a different endpoint: overall survival (post-randomization). See the Additional Endpoints per FDA Assessment section below for further details.
L186: ## Additional Endpoints per FDA Assessment
L187: This section lists additional endpoints used for regulatory consideration, as defined and adjudicated by FDA.
L188: ## **Event-Free Survival (post-transplantation)**
L189: We refer to the EFS (post-transplantation) herein as the endpoint with the same definition as the primary endpoint with several distinctions:
L190: **Day Zero:** Transplantation
L191: **Timeframe:** Unrestricted
L192: **Events:** Adjudicated by the FDA
L193: ## **Event-Free Survival (post-randomization)**
L194: We refer to the EFS (post-randomization) herein as the endpoint with the same definition as the primary endpoint with several distinctions:
L195: **Day Zero:** Randomization
L196: **Timeframe:** Unrestricted
L197: **Events:** Adjudicated by the FDA
L198: This was considered as the primary endpoint for review purposes. The FDA EFS adjudication process is summarized below.
L199: - All EFS events were adjudicated as defined in the protocol including use of the protocol definitions of relapse for AML, “relapse” of MDS (including relapse and progression), and graft failure (including primary and secondary GF).
- Patients without a prior event were censored at last follow up.
- The timing of events from PARAMCD=EFSSBITT in the updated adtte.xpt data file was compared to the source documentation.
- Relapse was confirmed based on the laboratory, bone marrow, and source documentation data resulting in updates to either the time of relapse or the occurrence of relapse for some patients.
- Review of the updated bone marrow data identified subjects whose baseline bone marrow results indicated bone marrow blast counts above the inclusion criterion for either AML or MDS. These patients were censored at randomization.
L200: ## ***Relapse-Free Survival (AML)***
L201: RFS was not an original trial endpoint. However, RFS is an acceptable regulatory endpoint in studies for treatments of patients with AML in remission. FDA defines RFS, for the purposes of this review, as the time from randomization to the date of morphologic relapse or death from any cause, whichever occurs first. In the study population with AML:
L202: - FDA adjudicated baseline remission status based on laboratory and bone marrow results as described above for EFS.
- The start date was revised to be date of randomization rather than end of HSCT.
- Censoring was applied at the time of events not included in FDA's definition of RFS (i.e., graft failure, cytogenetic relapse, molecular relapse) due to lack of consistent follow-up and documentation of morphologic relapse after these events had been called.
L203: ## ***Overall Survival (post-randomization)***
L204: For the regulatory consideration purposes, the overall survival is defined as the time from randomization until death due to any cause (not limited by a 24-month period). We refer to this FDA-defined endpoint as overall survival (post-randomization) herein.
L205: **Day Zero:** Randomization
L206: **Timeframe:** Unrestricted
L207: This is the main endpoint of interest from the regulatory perspective. No alpha was allocated, and no testing boundary was set for overall survival hypothesis testing. Please refer to the FDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation of NDA 214759 (30 July 2021) for further details.
L208: ## ***Analysis Populations***
L209: The protocol-specified analysis population for the primary analysis of EFS was the full analysis set (FAS), defined as all randomized patients who were treated at least one time with the study medication and with at least one documented efficacy parameter. Herein we will refer to all randomized patients as the intention-to-treat (ITT) analysis set.
L210: ## ***Statistical Analysis Plan***
L211: Please refer to the FDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation of NDA 214759 (30 July 2021). Per protocol, the primary analysis of EFS utilized a Cox model "(stratified by centre) with donor type (MUD vs. MRD), prognostic risk group (I vs. II) and treatment as factors". The primary analysis was intended to test non-inferiority based on EFS, with a non-inferiority margin of HR=1.3. Study MC-FLudT1.4 Trial II was designed with three interim analyses for efficacy and utility, planned to occur at 9.4%, 28.5%, and 49.7% information fraction (described in further detail in Section 8.1.2. The associated boundaries were based on the primary analysis of EFS, such that the trial would be stopped if efficacy or utility was met for the NI test of EFS.
L212: If the test of non-inferiority for EFS was found to be significant, a test of superiority would be conducted. Per protocol, "In case of statistical significance, superiority with respect to the primary study endpoint will be tested based on the points to consider on switching between superiority and noninferiority (CPMP/EWP/482/99)." This corresponds to a test of superiority at the alpha level utilized for the NI test.
L213: As noted above, the statistical analysis plan included center as one of the stratification factors. For the review purposes, this factor was excluded from the models used to assess additional endpoints per FDA assessment due to the excessive number of levels (See the Statistical Issues section for details).
L214: **Statistical Reviewer's Comment:** The 30 July 2021 FDA Multidisciplinary Review noted issues with the report submitted to justify the NI margin (inconsistency of EFS definition across studies, inconsistency of trial populations, lack of identification of a trial with a population exchangeable with Study MC-FludT1.4 Trial II). Furthermore, issues concerning the relevance of EFS in the enrolled population are detailed above. (b) (4)
L215: ## Protocol Amendments
L216: Please refer to the FDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation of NDA 214759 (30 July 2021).
L217: ## 8.1.2. Study Results
L218: ## Preamble:
L219: The following is a brief summary of the Study MC-FludT.14/L (Trial II) background, as described in the Clinical Study Report (v1.0, 5 March 2020):
L220: "The trial was planned as a group-sequential trial with 3 interim analyses. The first formal interim analysis was planned to be performed after 45 events or 220 patients to allow for a broad review of the benefits and risks of the dose reduction and change of the treatment regimen implemented with amendment 3 of the trial. Stopping early due to proof of efficacy or futility was unlikely within this interim analysis due to the low information fraction within this analysis. Further interim analyses were planned after 137 and 239 events occurred, or after 460 and 700 patients were randomised. The final analysis was planned after 481 events or inclusion of 930 patients at the latest.
L221: When reviewing the results of the second interim analysis, the DMC recommended to stop recruitment for this trial since the primary objective – the proof of non-inferiority of treosulfan compared to busulfan – had already been achieved. The previous CTR (V 1.0 dated 15-Sep-2017) described the results from the 476 patients available at that time and constitutes the confirmatory analysis of this trial. This updated report describes the results of the last analysis
L222: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation [NDA 214759]
L223: ## Grafapex (treosulfan)
L224: of all 570 patients enrolled in the trial including post surveillance data. Of these, 551 patients qualified for the FAS. In total, 234 events were observed representing an information fraction of 48.6% based on the 481 events planned for in the final analysis foreseen in the Clinical Trial Protocol if the trial had not been stopped prematurely."
L225: FDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation of NDA 214759 (30 July 2021) identified the following issues with the submitted data, among others, which resulted in a Complete Response:
L226: - "The primary endpoint of Study MC-FludT-14/L Trial II was event-free survival (EFS). Because all complete blood cell (CBC) and marrow results were not submitted, we were unable to independently confirm the results of the analysis of the primary endpoint of EFS for Study MC-FludT-14/L Trial II.
- Additionally, because of the missing CBC and marrow results, we were not able complete our assessment of whether the results of the study would support the indications for treatment of patients with AML and for treatment of patients with MDS."
L227: This review focuses on the evaluation of efficacy in Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II. The evaluation of efficacy relied on the additional data submitted with the application to address the deficiencies identified in the Complete Response. The FDA adjudication of the submitted data is summarized in Section 8: Statistical and Clinical and Evaluation.
L228: Study MC-FludT-14/L Trial II was stopped at the second interim analysis with 168 EFS events observed in the FAS population. A total of n=476 patients were randomized at this time; n=460 were included in the FAS. In a Type C meeting with FDA convened on 17 Sep 2019, FDA conveyed concerns regarding the low information fraction associated with the second interim analysis. FDA recommended the interim and final analyses be submitted for review. Following this meeting, the Applicant submitted a final CSR, dated 05 March 2020. The date of the last patient completed was 25 Jan 2018 in this report.
L229: Table 3 describes the two analysis populations (FAS and ITT) across the two reports.

L232: This review focuses on the last analysis of all 570 patients enrolled in the trial, unless stated otherwise.
L233: The key questions that this review of Study MC-FludT/14/Trial II is aiming to address are the following:
L234: - Has Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II reached its primary objective based on the event-free survival outcome, as adjudicated by the FDA?
- Has Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II demonstrated an advantage on overall survival and other efficacy outcomes for the treosulfan study group over the busulfan study group?
- Has Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II demonstrated favorable benefit-risk for the proposed indications for patients with AML and for patients with MDS?

L255: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L256: ## **EFS Adjudication**
L257: As noted above, the Applicant defined EFS as the time from transplant until an event of disease relapse, graft failure, or death, whichever occurs first. Disease relapse for AML was considered morphologic relapse, cytogenetic relapse, or molecular relapse. Disease relapse/progression for MDS was considered recurrence of marrow or peripheral blasts &gt;5% if they were &gt;5% at study entry and the patient had a marrow CR after transplant, reappearance of cytogenetic abnormalities with a \ge 5\% decline in donor chimerism, or reduction in peripheral counts (decrease of \ge 50\% from maximum response level (after engraftment) in granulocytes or platelets or reduction in Hgb concentration by \ge 1.5 g/dl from maximum response level (after engraftment) or transfusion dependency) accompanied by absolute decline in donor chimerism by \ge 5\%. The Applicant included either primary graft failure or secondary graft failure as an EFS event. During review of the initial submission, FDA attempted to verify if Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II met its primary endpoint as planned, completed, and reported by the Applicant. The clinical team noted that they required more comprehensive laboratory data and bone marrow data to complete an adjudication of the primary EFS endpoint as defined by the Applicant. Since this information was not initially collected as part of the study, the Applicant had to request the missing information from the individual study sites.
L258: The Applicant conducted an additional database lock on 16 Mar 2018 and characterized all EFS events observed (regardless of timing). Once collected, the Applicant utilized an external hematologist to adjudicate the EFS events based on the newly collected data (Table 5 below). FDA review noted two subjects with missed events. One patient had a missed secondary graft failure event that went on to receive a second transplant who had been previously censored at last follow up without an event. The other patient had a missed late relapse that had been previously censored at last follow up. The remaining six adjustments noted during the Applicant's review of the new data were to update the timing of relapse events based on the first evidence of relapse in the source documentation.

L265: With this current submission, FDA was able to review the source documentation for labs and bone marrow reports assembled by the Applicant and adjudicate EFS per the Applicant's definition. This included screening laboratory data for evidence of relapse or graft failure events and verifying that the data provided substantiates the occurrence and timing of EFS events. FDA agreed with the Applicant's EFS event updates in Table 5 above but noted 8 additional subjects that required adjudication (Table 6 below). Five patients required censoring from the analysis as source documentation (including translated bone marrow biopsy reports) revealed that their pretransplant disease assessment was not in range for inclusion. This meant that patients had bone marrow blasts \ge 5\% for AML patients or \ge 20\% for MDS patients. For two patients, FDA adjusted the timing of their relapse date based on review of the source data. Lastly, for one patient, FDA adjudicated their relapse event as not supported by source documentation with follow-up showing the patient was well and without relapse almost a year after the previously reported date. This patient was changed to censored at last follow up. These adjudications were submitted to the Applicant who agreed with FDA's justifications for the updates.

L280: ## ***Event-Free Survival (within 24 months post-transplantation)***
L281: The hazard ratio for EFS, as performed at the *interim* analysis in the FAS population with 168 events was 0.65 (95% CI: 0.47, 0.90). The p-value for non-inferiority testing was 0.0000164, which was significant as compared to the efficacy boundary of 0.000149. The p-value for superiority testing was 0.0051268, which did not cross the efficacy boundary. This endpoint had 224 events at the *last* analysis, which would correspond to a 46.6% information fraction. The efficacy boundary based on this information fraction would have been 0.00097. The hazard ratio for EFS, as defined in the protocol and performed at the *last* analysis, within 2 years post-transplantation, using Cox proportional hazards model with risk group and donor type, was 0.64 (95% CI: 0.49, 0.83), p-value (one-sided) for superiority was 0.0004 (efficacy stopping boundary was 0.00097).
L282: ***Statistical Reviewer's Comment:*** The 224 events corresponding to the “*last*” analysis are based on additional events observed for the primary endpoint (EFS within 24 months post-transplantation). While this endpoint met the superiority boundary at this time, this does not account for the previous analysis, though the alpha spent at the prior analyses was small. This boundary is hypothetical – it corresponds to a trial which would have conducted an interim analysis at this time. This analysis was not planned but requested by FDA. While the boundary is useful for providing context for the p-value, it does not represent the traditional boundary utilized for demonstrating efficacy.
L283: ## ***Event-Free Survival (post-transplantation)***
L284: FDA conducted additional analyses on EFS to assess its robustness to 1) adjudication of events and 2) the 2-year analysis window. These analyses use data from the final CSR, which includes events occurring after the 2-year analysis window. The analyses that follow do not use site as a stratification factor, as this resulted in a high number of strata (site/center factor has 22 levels).
L285: In the final CSR date 16 Mar 2018, the Applicant reported the hazard ratio for EFS (post-transplantation), unrestricted to any period, as 0.65 (95% CI: 0.49, 0.84). With FDA adjudication, the hazard ratio for EFS (post-transplantation), unrestricted to any period, was 0.65 (95% CI: 0.50, 0.85), p-value (one-sided) was 0.0007 (Table 9).
L286: Table 9 shows the summaries. These EFS events include post-surveillance events, i.e., those which occur beyond 2 years post-HSCT. Per the final CSR, this represents “an information fraction of 48.6% based on the 481 events planned for in the final analysis foreseen in the Clinical Trial Protocol if the trial had not been stopped prematurely. The information fraction of 48.6% corresponds to an event-driven alpha level of 0.001262 within this last analysis according to the O’Brien-Fleming \alpha-spending function.” The database lock for the last analysis occurred on 16 March 2018.

L294: **Statistical Reviewer's Comment:** The 234 EFS events referenced above include events beyond 2 years post-HSCT, which is inconsistent with the primary endpoint of Study MC-FludT-14/L Trial II. The reported information fraction of 48.6% is not representative of how this quantity is typically reported; therefore, the associated boundary has no clear meaning. This percentage might be used to contextualize how much additional information was encoded in the additional events included in the final CSR, although this may be misleading. For instance, had the trial been designed for EFS at any time, the targeted final number of events may have been different. For these reasons, the p-value and boundary above should not be interpreted as demonstrating
L295: *efficacy based on this endpoint. This trial was not designed for this definition of EFS, nor was the associated boundary.*

L299: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L300: ## **Overall Survival (within 24 months post-transplantation)**
L301: The HR for overall survival (within 24 months post-transplantation) in the FAS population reported at the *interim* analysis was 0.61 (95% CI: 0.42, 0.88) with 82/240 (34%) of events in the busulfan group and 52/220 (24%) of events in the treosulfan group (adjusted for donor type as factor, and risk group and center as strata using Cox regression model). This analysis was included in the interim CSR.
L302: The HR for overall survival (within 24 months post-transplantation) in the FAS population at the *last* analysis was 0.63 (95% CI: 0.47, 0.86) with 107/283 (38%) of events in the busulfan group and 72/268 (27%) events and in the treosulfan group (adjusted for donor type and risk group as stratification factors). Seventy-three percent (95% CI: 67%, 78%) of patients in the treosulfan
L303: group and 60% (95% CI: 54%, 66%) of patients in the busulfan group were alive at 24 months. The results suggest an OS advantage in the treosulfan group as compared to the busulfan group. This endpoint was not specified in the analysis plan to be formally tested.
L304: ## Dose/Dose Response
L305: There was no exploration of dose/dose response in this study. See discussion of dose/response in Section 8.1.3 and in the FDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation of NDA 214759 (30 July 2021).

L312: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L313: ## *Event-Free Survival (post-randomization)*
L314: The hazard ratio for EFS (post-randomization) in the ITT population was 0.65 (95% CI: 0.50, 0.85), p-value (one-sided) is 0.0006. Table 10 and Figure 2 show the summaries. The one-sided significance level resulting from an event-driven O'Brien-Fleming type group-sequential efficacy stopping boundary was 0.001262.
L315: ***Statistical Reviewer's Comment:*** *The p-value and boundary above should not be interpreted as demonstrating efficacy based on this endpoint. As noted above, this trial was not designed for this definition of EFS, nor was the associated boundary. However, this boundary may provide useful context for interpreting the results of this endpoint.*

L326: **Clinical Reviewer's Comment:** As shown above, the adjudicated EFS results analyzed by FDA either within 24 months of transplant or with an unrestricted timeframe resulted in results which were consistent with the per protocol definition and analysis of EFS. While Study MC FludT.14/L Trial II met its primary analysis of EFS, the use of an EFS endpoint that comprised nonstandard EFS events of unclear clinical benefit made these results unsuitable (b) (4). It is important to note that a properly defined EFS endpoint could support findings of efficacy for AML with active disease (U.S. Food and Drug Administration, 2022). However, since the patients with AML enrolled on Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II were in remission, an RFS endpoint would better capture the clinical benefit in this context (because failing to achieve remission was not a risk for these patients and is a key event for an EFS endpoint). FDA analyzed RFS for patients with AML in an exploratory analysis detailed below. For patients with MDS, while recent criteria propose a definition for EFS in MDS, the clinical meaningfulness of these definitions have not been established. Available data indicate that CR remains the response endpoint associated with the greatest long-term benefit, and a definition of EFS that captures failure to achieve remission might be an acceptable endpoint. However, the protocol EFS definition did not include failure to achieve remission as a component of treatment failure and the frequency of CBC and bone marrow data collection did not allow for reliable assessment of remission status. Without this remission assessment, it is less clear how to interpret subsequent disease relapse/progression events. Ultimately, the OS best captured clinical benefit of treosulfan as discussed below.

L369: Database lock date: 16 March 2018
L370: Source: FDA Analysis
L371: **Clinical Reviewer's Comment on MRD vs MUD:** As noted in the analysis above and during the initial review of the data, the benefit demonstrated in the OS endpoint appears to be driven by the patients utilizing a MUD for transplant. It is not clear why this might have been observed, but it is most likely that this apparent difference is a chance finding since the imbalance in the number of events was small. Clinically, patients receiving a transplant from a MUD versus MRD have differing competing risks post-transplant of GVHD, infection, and relapse that are all affected by the different donor type. There will be a higher risk of GVHD with MUD but greater chance of a graft versus leukemia effect (lower relapse chance). Moreover, the need for anti-thymocyte globulin (ATG) to be added to the transplant conditioning regimen of MUD transplants to reduce the higher risk of GVHD is at the expense of increased infectious risk and the potential for a decreased graft versus leukemia effect. Within these competing and varying risks, it is unclear how the use of treosulfan versus busulfan would have further altered these risks to only benefit the patients receiving a MUD transplant. However, it is important to
L372: *consider that the trial was not designed to test treosulfan against a placebo control; rather it was designed to show the efficacy of treosulfan as an alkylating agent used in conditioning compared to an active control arm of busulfan conditioning.*

L392: ## ***Subgroup Analyses: Relapse-Free Survival (AML Population)***
L393: FDA conducted an ad hoc analysis of RFS in patients with AML. As noted above, a properly defined EFS endpoint could support findings of efficacy for either AML or MDS. However, since patients with AML enrolled on Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II were in remission, an RFS endpoint better captures the potential benefit in this clinical context (because failing to achieve remission, which is a key event for an EFS endpoint, was not a risk for patients already in remission). Additionally, the Applicant's inclusion of graft failure as an EFS event complicates interpretation of the EFS endpoint. Graft failure is assessed in all trials concerning HSCT; however, graft failure is considered a safety issue since this is a complication of the transplant procedure.
L394: RFS events for AML were adjudicated by FDA based on the EFS adjudications noted above. RFS was defined as the time from randomization until morphologic relapse or death from any cause, whichever comes first. The Applicant included three criteria for AML relapse in their EFS definition: hematologic, cytogenetic, and molecular. However, only hematologic relapse criteria are fit for regulatory considerations. Moreover, investigation of the data showed that little follow up data was available for patients once an event of molecular or cytogenetic relapse was called. Similar issues with lack of follow up data were noted for patients with primary or secondary graft failure. Therefore, to achieve the EFS to RFS adjudication in the AML population, FDA censored patients at the time of molecular or cytogenetic relapse and primary or secondary graft failure since follow up data for morphological relapse was not routinely collected after these events were called. This is shown conceptually in Table 15 and with detailed patient adjudications in Table 16 below. The Applicant agreed with FDA adjudications for the AML RFS analysis.
L395: Table 15. FDA's EFS to RFS Event Censoring Procedure

L412: ## 8.1.3 Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L413: ## Methods
L414: The Applicant proposed an indication for treosulfan "for use in combination with fludarabine as a preparative regimen for allogeneic hematopoietic stem cell transplantation (alloHSCT) in adult and pediatric patients &gt; 1 year old with acute myeloid leukemia (AML)" and "...with myelodysplastic syndrome (MDS)." The proposed dose of treosulfan was of 10 g/m2/dose for use in the combination described.
L415: The clinical development program in AML and MDS included two randomized control trials, three single-arm trials, one dose-escalation study, and two registry studies (See Table 35 in FDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation of NDA 214759 (30 July 2021)). All clinical trials were conducted outside the US. The pivotal trial MC-FludT.14/L Trial II was relevant to determining efficacy in the intended population. In addition, MC-FludT.14/L Trial I and MC-FludT.6/L were used to support the proposed 10 g/m2 treosulfan dose. Other studies included in the submission were reviewed but were not considered to have contributed to assessment of efficacy for the proposed indications. See FDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation of NDA 214759 (30 July 2021) for full list of studies and limitations of those studies.
L416: ## MC-FludT.14/L Trial II
L417: ## Overview
L418: The Applicant proposed to use Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II (Trial 14 - II; treosulfan 10 g/m2/dose) to support the proposed indication. Trial 14 - II was a multicenter, randomized, active-controlled trial comparing treosulfan to busulfan in combination with fludarabine as a preparative regimen for allogeneic HSCT. Eligible patients included adults 18 to 70 years old with AML or MDS, Karnofsky performance status &gt; 60%, and age ≥ 50 years or hematopoietic cell transplantation comorbidity index [HCT CI] score &gt; 2. The patients were randomized to receive treosulfan 10 g/m2 daily on days -4, -3 and -2 or to busulfan 0.8 mg/kg every 6 hours on days -4 and -3 in combination with fludarabine 30 mg/m2 daily on days -6, -5, -4, -3 and -2, and hematopoietic stem cell transplantation on day 0. Antithymocyte globulin was administered as well to patients with unrelated donors.
L419: In general, a randomized controlled trial with a survival-based endpoint is appropriate to support an indication for a drug in a preparative regimen for allogeneic HSCT. FDA frequently requires two trials to determine efficacy, but one trial might be sufficient if the trial is adequately designed and well-conducted, and the results are internally consistent, clinically meaningful, and statistically persuasive.
L420: ## Overview of Issues with Data Collection
L421: The following issues regarding data collection were noted in the FDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation of NDA 214759 (30 July 2021):
L422: - "The primary endpoint of Study MC-FludT-14/L Trial II was event-free survival (EFS). Because all complete blood cell (CBC) and marrow results were not submitted, we were unable to independently confirm the results of the analysis of the primary endpoint of EFS for Study MC-FludT-14/L Trial II.
- Additionally, because of the missing CBC and marrow results, we were not able to complete our assessment of whether the results of the study would support the indications for treatment of patients with AML and for treatment of patients with MDS."
L423: Additional data were submitted by the Applicant in the current resubmission. FDA adjudicated time-to-event outcomes based on these data. Relapse was confirmed based on the laboratory, bone marrow, and source documentation data resulting in updates to either the time of relapse or the occurrence of relapse for some patients.
L424: The extent of the submitted data is limited. Per Complete Response to the FDA Complete Response Letter 30 July 2021, page 10: "... the collection of some of the marrow data was considered not mandatory under the study protocol MC-FludT.14/L Trial II, particularly beyond Day +100 and in case of lack of an event. However, in some instances, bone marrow was collected beyond Day +100 and details of these assessments are provided." Given the lack of reliable data collection beyond Day 100 (and "in case of lack of an event"), outcomes relying on such data beyond Day 100 might be challenging to interpret.
L425: ## Overview of Issues with Trial Design
L426: FDA noted several issues with the design of Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II. These include:
L427: - Inclusion of two distinct clinical populations, MDS and AML, without a pre-specified plan to formally test each subpopulation.
- The definition of the primary endpoint EFS, which included non-efficacy events such as graft failure and whose applicability to the AML and MDS subpopulations is unclear.
- The primary objective of non-inferiority for EFS, with a NI margin lacking sufficient justification.
- Unusual choices for the primary analysis, including use of an analysis population of FAS rather than the ITT and inclusion of a high number of stratification levels (due to inclusion of site as a stratification factor).
- Lack of a pre-specified testing plan for OS.
- Multiple early interim analyses for efficacy which limited the overall available information on secondary endpoints.
L428: - Study conduct based solely on the NI test of EFS, which precluded further enrollment and follow-up for other key analyses and endpoints (e.g., the superiority test of EFS and analyses related to OS).
- Per the unireview dated 30 Jul 2021 regarding assessment of response in patients with MDS: “results of marrow examinations which would allow for assessment of response were not collected systematically, so response cannot be assessed.”
L429: ## Overview of Endpoint Definitions
L430: Table 18 shows differences in definitions for the main outcomes of interest. FDA relied primarily on the outcomes listed in the *Definitions per FDA* column for the benefit-risk assessment. Furthermore, while Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II utilized the FAS for efficacy analyses, FDA relied on the ITT population and subsets thereof (namely, the AML and MDS subpopulations of the ITT).

L434: FDA used a modified statistical analysis plan for assessing time-to-event outcomes. The results reported herein use a Cox proportional hazards model stratified by donor type and risk group only. See Section 8.3: Statistical Issues for further details.

L441: Source: FDA Analysis
L442: The results show favorable outcomes for treosulfan compared to busulfan in the ITT population across multiple efficacy endpoints as discussed below.
L443: ## **Primary Endpoint**
L444: ## ***Event-Free Survival***
L445: Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II was stopped early for efficacy at 35% (168/481 planned events in the FAS population) information fraction of EFS. Early stopping for efficacy might result in an overestimation of a treatment effect. The hazard ratio for EFS, as performed at the *interim* analysis was 0.65 (95% CI: 0.47, 0.90). The p-value for non-inferiority testing was 0.0000164, which was significant as compared to the efficacy boundary of 0.000149. The p-value for superiority testing was 0.0051268, which did not cross the efficacy boundary. Results for EFS at the *last* analysis suggested the p-value would have crossed the corresponding superiority boundary, though this analysis was ad-hoc and the corresponding boundary hypothetical in nature.
L446: Per the statistical analysis plan, the primary efficacy endpoint was “event-free survival (EFS) within 2 years after transplantation measured from time of end of HSCT (= day 0) to time of event. The associated time span is defined as the interval from day 0 to relapse, graft failure or death (whatever occurs first). The date of the event is either the documented date of relapse/progression or the date of death or the date of primary (date of examination of neutrophilic granulocytes) or secondary graft failure (date of examination of neutrophilic granulocytes).” This is not a standard definition used for regulatory considerations in AML or MDS populations. Per FDA's Acute Myeloid Leukemia: Developing Drugs and Biological Products for Treatment Guidance for Industry, EFS is defined as the “time from randomization to the date of: induction treatment failure, relapse for those who have induction treatment success (e.g., complete remission), or death from any cause, whichever comes first”. Interpretation of the results based on the protocol-defined EFS outcome may be challenging.
L447: **Has Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II reached its primary objective based on the event-free survival outcome, as adjudicated by the FDA?**
L448: Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II reached its primary objective of demonstrating that EFS (post-transplantation, within 2 years) outcomes were non-inferior on the treosulfan arm compared to the busulfan arm. Data were submitted in support of adjudication of this endpoint for this submission. After adjudication, FDA found that the results for EFS as originally reported were largely consistent with those that were adjudicated.
L449: Results were similar for other definitions of EFS. The hazard ratio for EFS (post-randomization, without time restriction) was 0.65 (95% CI: 0.50, 0.85), indicative of a positive treatment effect of treosulfan compared to busulfan.
L450: As noted above, EFS as defined per protocol includes both safety and efficacy events and is not aligned with FDA guidance. Furthermore, FDA guidance for the treatment of AML states that EFS may be considered as an efficacy endpoint in populations not in remission, but that RFS is the appropriate for populations that are in remission at baseline. As the AML subpopulation was in remission at baseline and the MDS subpopulation was not, any conceptual “event-free” survival endpoint would likely differ for these two subpopulations. Therefore, EFS results in the enrolled population has no clear interpretation. (b) (4)
L451: ## Secondary and Other Endpoints
L452: Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II was stopped at the second interim analysis based on a significant test of EFS non-inferiority. While superiority of EFS was an alpha-controlled, secondary objective, the associated statistical test did not meet significance at the second interim analysis. Furthermore, OS was not specified as an endpoint to be tested. For trials with important secondary objectives, a common design is one which continues to follow for the secondary objective even if the primary objective is met. This approach was not utilized in Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II. Thus, the results of secondary endpoints must be considered in light of these limitations.
L453: ## Overall Survival
L454: For Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II, the analysis of OS was considered exploratory. FDA assessed OS in the ITT population with using the full follow-up period. No alpha-controlled hypothesis testing was prespecified for the overall survival or for testing AML and MDS subgroups. Not controlling for multiplicity might result in erroneous conclusions.
L455: Per statistical analysis plan, OS is defined as “the time length between day 0 and the date of death due to any cause within the 24-month study period (i.e. excluding post-surveillance evaluation).” Day zero is the time of allogeneic hematopoietic stem cell transplantation. This is not a standard OS definition used for regulatory purposes, which typically uses 1) randomization as the start of the period of interest 2) has no restriction on follow-up (i.e., no limit set to 24 months). Since the OS is both safety and efficacy endpoint, the regulatory interest is in the full unrestricted survival period starting from randomization, referred to OS (post-randomization) herein.
L456: ## **Has Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II demonstrated an advantage on overall survival and other efficacy outcomes for the treosulfan study group over the busulfan study group?**
L457: Table 19 shows hazard ratios and 95% confidence intervals for the OS in the ITT population as well as in AML and MDS populations separately. These results are based on the final CSR with database lock 16 Mar 2018. Based on the reverse Kaplan-Meier estimate, median follow-up time for OS (post-randomization) was 29.6 months (range: 0, 54.5) in the busulfan group and 29.5 months (range: 0, 52.5) in the treosulfan group. The hazard ratio for OS (post-randomization) was 0.67 (95% CI: 0.51, 0.90) in the ITT population, demonstrating an OS advantage in the treosulfan group compared to the busulfan group. The upper bounds of the 95% confidence intervals slightly exceed one in the AML and in the MDS subgroups, suggesting that some residual uncertainty regarding efficacy in these subgroups remains. Nonetheless, the results for patients with AML and MDS are consistent with the overall results of the study.
L458: In Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II, treatment with treosulfan-based conditioning resulted in improved OS compared to busulfan-based conditioning in the ITT population. Since FDA verified that Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II was a positive study based on its EFS primary endpoint, FDA could consider the OS endpoint as an acceptable endpoint for assessment of the efficacy of treosulfan for use in transplant conditioning. Unlike EFS, OS was not subject to the same interpretation issues based on the events included in the EFS definition and incomplete capture of follow up monitoring. Moreover, since monitoring for OS is simpler, there are fewer concerns for missed events compared to an EFS endpoint. The timing of OS events is unambiguous compared to EFS events and because patients can be reliably censored at the time of last follow up/know well without concern for a missed event occurring prior to that last follow up encounter.
L459: Overall survival is both safety and efficacy endpoint and remains the gold standard in clinical trials. The results show a substantial treatment effect on the overall survival in the full study population. However, alpha-controlled hypothesis testing was not prespecified. Therefore, p-values should not be included in the USPI. To communicate the full survival functions in treosulfan and busulfan groups in patients with AML and, separately, in patients with MDS, including Kaplan-Meier plots in the USPI is recommended, along with the treatment effect point estimates and confidence intervals.
L460: ## **Additional Efficacy Endpoints**
L461: The Applicant prespecified several secondary endpoints discussed in the original review, but these largely consisted of safety endpoints or endpoints that could not be used in support of the OS results to demonstrate the effectiveness of treosulfan for treatment of AML or MDS.
L462: For treatment of patients with AML transplanted in remission, RFS would be the usual endpoint to support the OS findings. To investigate RFS in Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II, FDA reviewed the available EFS data and determined how to appropriately convert the Applicant's EFS analysis into a traditional RFS analysis. This showed that the hazard ratio for RFS in the AML patients
L463: was 0.72 (95% CI: 0.51, 1.03). This is also well-captured by the RFS at 24 months which showed estimates of 59% (95% CI: 50%, 55%) versus 69% (95% CI: 61%, 75%) for busulfan and treosulfan, respectively. This suggests that when focused on the RFS events of hematologic relapse or death, that treatment with treosulfan showed a lower risk of these events compared to treatment with busulfan further supporting the findings of efficacy in the AML population demonstrated in the OS analysis.
L464: For treatment of MDS not in remission, EFS (defined appropriately) or CR that is durable might be suitable to assess activity. However, because CBC and marrow examination data were not collected systematically to assess achievement of remission in this protocol, achievement of CR and/or treatment failure as an event for EFS cannot be assessed.
L465: ## Subpopulations
L466: ## Has Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II demonstrated favorable benefit-risk for the proposed indications for patients with AML and for patients with MDS?
L467: ## Disease
L468: FDA considers treatment of AML and MDS to be two distinct indications which would typically warrant separate sources of substantial evidence of effectiveness to support approval. Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II enrolled both populations with no plan to formally assess them separately. In addition, the sample size for each subpopulation was not based on power considerations or to ensure a certain number of patients in each subgroup. These features limited the ability Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II to establish substantial evidence for either disease alone.
L469: However, FDA considered it may be reasonable to assess efficacy in a combined ITT population of AML and MDS provided the endpoint used had equivalent interpretation for both diseases, which the OS analysis provided. For trials with such mixed populations, it is critical that a sufficient number of patients in each subpopulation be enrolled so that differential treatment effects can be identified and described. This appears to have been the case for Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II, which enrolled 365 patients with AML and 205 patients with MDS.
L470: As noted above, the definition and relevance of EFS to each setting is unclear. While the treatment effect on EFS appears to be similar in the two subpopulations, FDA does not consider this as direct evidence of effectiveness overall or for either subpopulation. FDA found the OS results to be similar across the two populations, with a hazard ratio of 0.73 (95% CI: 0.51, 1.06) for the AML population and 0.64 (95% CI: 0.40, 1.02) for the MDS population (Table 19). Taken together, these observations suggest 1) that the positive result observed in Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II was not driven by either the AML or MDS subgroup and 2) that the observed treatment effect on OS was not driven by either the AML or MDS subgroup.
L471: Given these considerations, FDA considers Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II to have demonstrated
L472: favorable benefit-risk for the proposed indications for patients with AML and for patients with MDS.
L473: ## Race and Ethnicity
L474: Data on race and ethnicity were not collected for any of the studies included in this NDA. This deficiency leaves some uncertainty about the generalizability of the efficacy outcomes with respect to the US population. While the results from the European region of multiregional trials have generally been similar to North American results (Wilson, J NCCN, 2022) and, based on treosulfan's mechanism of action as an alkylating agent, there is no reason to believe that the benefit-risk profile demonstrated in Study MC-FludT.14/L Trial II would be expected to differ by racial or ethnic background, the lack of racial or ethnic reporting in this application did not allow for assessment of efficacy or safety results by race and ethnicity. Additional information is needed to ensure there are no unexpected differences in the benefit-risk profile in US racial and ethnic groups. To address this, the Applicant has agreed to assess the PK and exposure profile and registry-based outcomes by race and ethnicity in PMC #1 and PMC #2, respectively.
L475: ## Donor Type
L476: In MC-FludT.14/L Trial II, 76% of patients had unrelated donors and 24% had related donors. Table 20 shows OS (post-randomization) by donor type.
